\Hypertarget{vmalloc_8c_a02b14794ec966816ffe397474b985361}\index{vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}!fit\_type@{fit\_type}}
\index{fit\_type@{fit\_type}!vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{fit\_type}{fit\_type}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a02b14794ec966816ffe397474b985361} 
enum \doxymbox{\hyperlink{vmalloc_8c_a02b14794ec966816ffe397474b985361}{fit\+\_\+type}}}

Enumeration Description:\+ This enumeration defines how a requested allocation fits within a free \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area}. NOTHING\+\_\+\+FIT means the request does not fit at all. FL\+\_\+\+FIT\+\_\+\+TYPE (full fit) means the request exactly matches the entire \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area}. LE\+\_\+\+FIT\+\_\+\+TYPE (left edge fit) means the request aligns with the left edge of the area, leaving free space on the right. RE\+\_\+\+FIT\+\_\+\+TYPE (right edge fit) means the request aligns with the right edge, leaving free space on the left. NE\+\_\+\+FIT\+\_\+\+TYPE (no edge fit) means the request fits entirely within the middle of the area, requiring splitting on both sides. This classification is used during allocation to determine how to clip and split the \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area}. \begin{DoxyEnumFields}[2]{Enumerator}

Merge de-\/allocated chunk of VA memory with previous and next free blocks. If coalesce is not done a new free area is inserted. If VA has been merged, it is freed.

Please note, it can return NULL in case of overlap ranges, followed by WARN() report. Despite it is a buggy behaviour, a system can be alive and keep ongoing.

Function Description:\+ Merges a \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} with adjacent free areas or inserts it into the free tree. It coalesces with previous and next free blocks. Returns the merged/\+inserted area or NULL on overlap.

Purges all lazily-\/freed vmap areas.

Function Description:\+ Purges all lazily-\/freed vmap areas. It processes all nodes, decays pools, and schedules purge work. Returns true if any areas were purged.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\_\_vmalloc\_node\_noprof()}{\_\_vmalloc\_node\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_af101d51085d249f99c8a0812d815fa9a} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} \+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{align}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfp\+\_\+mask}{, }\item[{int}]{node}{, }\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{caller}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node -\/ allocate virtually contiguous memory @size:\+ allocation size @align:\+ desired alignment @gfp\+\_\+mask:\+ flags for the page level allocator @node:\+ node to use for allocation or NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE @caller:\+ caller\textquotesingle{}s return address

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator with @gfp\+\_\+mask flags. Map them into contiguous kernel virtual space.

Semantics of @gfp\+\_\+mask (including reclaim/\+retry modifiers such as \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOFAIL) are the same as in \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a699b8df354004dc37ced2903d4833a54}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range\+\_\+noprof()}.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL on error

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory on a specific node. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a699b8df354004dc37ced2903d4833a54}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range\+\_\+noprof()} with VMALLOC\+\_\+\+START/\+\+END. Returns the address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory on a specific node with alignment without allocation profiling. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_adcb6460c4975e163db21e9c8d6adb01e}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node()}.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\_\_vmalloc\_node\_range\_noprof()}{\_\_vmalloc\_node\_range\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_aecfaafb3ba16c7feec7f449824ddea5c} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} \+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{align}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{start}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{end}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfp\+\_\+mask}{, }\item[{pgprot\+\_\+t}]{prot}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{vm\+\_\+flags}{, }\item[{int}]{node}{, }\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{caller}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range -\/ allocate virtually contiguous memory @size:\+ allocation size @align:\+ desired alignment @start:\+ vm area range start @end:\+ vm area range end @gfp\+\_\+mask:\+ flags for the page level allocator @prot:\+ protection mask for the allocated pages @vm\+\_\+flags:\+ additional vm area flags (e.\+g. VM\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+GUARD) @node:\+ node to use for allocation or NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE @caller:\+ caller\textquotesingle{}s return address

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator with @gfp\+\_\+mask flags and map them into contiguous virtual range with protection @prot.

Supported GFP classes:\+ GFP\+\_\+\+KERNEL, GFP\+\_\+\+ATOMIC, GFP\+\_\+\+NOWAIT, GFP\+\_\+\+NOFS and GFP\+\_\+\+NOIO. Zone modifiers are not supported. Please note GFP\+\_\+\+ATOMIC and GFP\+\_\+\+NOWAIT are supported only by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_abf4d6ac386bd660cbdd449b5cfefc11d}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc()}.

Retry modifiers:\+ only \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOFAIL is supported; \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NORETRY and \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+RETRY\+\_\+\+MAYFAIL are not supported.

\+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOWARN can be used to suppress failure messages.

Can not be called from interrupt nor NMI contexts. Return:\+ the address of the area or NULL on failure

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory in a range. It handles alignment, huge pages, KASAN, and retries. This is the core vmalloc allocation function.

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory within a specific address range without allocation profiling. It allows specifying start and end addresses, alignment, flags, protection, and node. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a08b352ce7783e3a20c50e7a6e8fcd612}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range()}.

This is only for performance analysis of vmalloc and stress purpose. It is required by vmalloc test module, therefore do not use it other than that. Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory with a gfp mask. It fixes invalid flags and calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_ac0437723f499fd249a1859f24eff01d0}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+noprof()}. Returns the address or NULL.

This is an internal function only. Do not use outside mm/\+.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a56faacb795fb5550aebd655c7b43bb7e} 
void \+\_\+\+\_\+vunmap\+\_\+range\+\_\+noflush (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{start}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{end}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vunmap\+\_\+range\+\_\+noflush is similar to vunmap\+\_\+range, but does not flush caches or TLBs.

The caller is responsible for calling flush\+\_\+cache\+\_\+vmap() before calling this function, and flush\+\_\+tlb\+\_\+kernel\+\_\+range after it has returned successfully (and before the addresses are expected to cause a page fault or be re-\/mapped for something else, if TLB flushes are being delayed or coalesced).

This is an internal function only. Do not use outside mm/\+.

Function Description:\+ Internal function that unmaps a range of virtual addresses without flushing caches or TLBs. It walks the page table hierarchy and clears mappings. The caller is responsible for cache and TLB flushing.

We should probably have a fallback mechanism to allocate virtual memory out of partially filled vmap blocks. However vmap block sizing should be fairly reasonable according to the vmalloc size, so it shouldn\textquotesingle{}t be a big problem.

Function Description:\+ Converts a virtual address to a \doxylink{structvmap__block}{vmap\+\_\+block} index within the VMAP\+\_\+\+BLOCK\+\_\+\+SIZE-\/aligned space. \doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{addr\_to\_vb\_xa()}{addr\_to\_vb\_xa()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_aaeed9e61e7ffabaaebb7ebd8050519c1} 
struct xarray \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} addr\+\_\+to\+\_\+vb\+\_\+xa (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{addr}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

In order to fast access to any "{}vmap\+\_\+block"{} associated with a specific address, we use a hash.

A per-\/cpu \doxylink{structvmap__block__queue}{vmap\+\_\+block\+\_\+queue} is used in both ways, to serialize an access to free block chains among CPUs(alloc path) and it also acts as a \doxylink{structvmap__block}{vmap\+\_\+block} hash(alloc/\+free paths). It means we overload it, since we already have the per-\/cpu array which is used as a hash table. When used as a hash a \textquotesingle{}cpu\textquotesingle{} passed to per\+\_\+cpu() is not actually a CPU but rather a hash index.

A hash function is \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_aaeed9e61e7ffabaaebb7ebd8050519c1}{addr\+\_\+to\+\_\+vb\+\_\+xa()} which hashes any address to a specific index(in a hash) it belongs to. This then uses a per\+\_\+cpu() macro to access an array with generated index.

An example:\+

CPU\+\_\+1 CPU\+\_\+2 CPU\+\_\+0 \texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|} \texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|} \texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|} V V V 0 10 20 30 40 50 60 \texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}-\/-\/-\/---\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}-\/-\/-\/---\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}-\/-\/-\/---\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}-\/-\/-\/---\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}-\/-\/-\/---\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}-\/-\/-\/---\texorpdfstring{$\vert$}{|}...$<$vmap address space$>$ CPU0 CPU1 CPU2 CPU0 CPU1 CPU2


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item CPU\+\_\+1 invokes vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+ram(6), 6 belongs to CPU0 zone, thus it access:\+ CPU0/\+\+INDEX0 -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>} vmap\+\_\+blocks -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>} xa\+\_\+lock;
\item CPU\+\_\+2 invokes vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+ram(11), 11 belongs to CPU1 zone, thus it access:\+ CPU1/\+\+INDEX1 -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>} vmap\+\_\+blocks -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>} xa\+\_\+lock;
\item CPU\+\_\+0 invokes vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+ram(20), 20 belongs to CPU2 zone, thus it access:\+ CPU2/\+\+INDEX2 -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>} vmap\+\_\+blocks -\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>} xa\+\_\+lock.
\end{DoxyItemize}

This technique almost always avoids lock contention on insert/\+remove, however xarray spinlocks protect against any contention that remains.

Function Description:\+ Converts a virtual address to the xarray containing vmap\+\_\+blocks for that address. It calculates a hash index by dividing the address by VMAP\+\_\+\+BLOCK\+\_\+\+SIZE and taking modulo nr\+\_\+cpu\+\_\+ids. If the resulting index is not a possible CPU, it finds the next possible CPU. This hashing technique distributes vmap\+\_\+blocks across per-\/\+CPU xarrays to reduce lock contention. Returns a pointer to the xarray for the corresponding \doxylink{structvmap__block__queue}{vmap\+\_\+block\+\_\+queue}. 4MB with 4K pages

Returns the number of copied bytes.

Allocate a region of KVA of the specified size and alignment, within the vstart and vend. If vm is passed in, the two will also be bound.

Function Description:\+ Allocates a \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} from the global KVA space. It handles both pool and tree allocation, retries on failure, and supports blocking/\+non-\/blocking modes. Returns the \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} or an error pointer.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{augment\_tree\_propagate\_from()}{augment\_tree\_propagate\_from()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a0bbc43470e22c897a68ddab793976078} 
\+\_\+\+\_\+always\+\_\+inline void augment\+\_\+tree\+\_\+propagate\+\_\+from (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{va}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

This function populates subtree\+\_\+max\+\_\+size from bottom to upper levels starting from VA point. The propagation must be done when VA size is modified by changing its va\+\_\+start/\+va\+\_\+end. Or in case of newly inserting of VA to the tree.

It means that \+\_\+\+\_\+augment\+\_\+tree\+\_\+propagate\+\_\+from() must be called:\+
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item After VA has been inserted to the tree(free path);
\item After VA has been shrunk(allocation path);
\item After VA has been increased(merging path).
\end{DoxyItemize}

Please note that, it does not mean that upper parent nodes and their subtree\+\_\+max\+\_\+size are recalculated all the time up to the root node. \begin{DoxyVerb}  4--8
   /\
  /  \
 /    \
\end{DoxyVerb}
 2--2 8--8

For example if we modify the node 4, shrinking it to 2, then no any modification is required. If we shrink the node 2 to 1 its subtree\+\_\+max\+\_\+size is updated only, and set to 1. If we shrink the node 8 to 6, then its subtree\+\_\+max\+\_\+size is set to 6 and parent node becomes 4--6.

Function Description:\+ Propagates subtree\+\_\+max\+\_\+size values up the red-\/black tree from a given node. It updates the maximum free block size for all ancestors. Used after modifications to the free tree.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{encode\_vn\_id()}{encode\_vn\_id()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a47d5f3e3dae5cbd951f0e7123af3320a} 
unsigned int encode\+\_\+vn\+\_\+id (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned int}]{node\+\_\+id}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

We use the value 0 to represent "{}no node"{}, that is why an encoded value will be the node-\/id incremented by 1. It is always greater then 0. A valid node\+\_\+id which can be encoded is \+[0:\+nr\+\_\+vmap\+\_\+nodes -\/ 1]\+. If a passed node\+\_\+id is not valid 0 is returned.

Function Description:\+ Encodes a node ID into a value that can be stored in \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} flags. It increments the node ID by 1 and shifts it by 8 bits. Returns 0 if the node ID is invalid.

This function returns back addresses of parent node and its left or right link for further processing.

Otherwise NULL is returned. In that case all further steps regarding inserting of conflicting overlap range have to be declined and actually considered as a bug.

Function Description:\+ Finds the insertion point for a \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} in a red-\/black tree. It searches for the correct position and returns the link pointer and parent. Returns NULL if an overlap is detected.

find\+\_\+vm\+\_\+area -\/ find a continuous kernel virtual area @addr:\+ base address

Search for the kernel VM area starting at @addr, and return it. It is up to the caller to do all required locking to keep the returned pointer valid.

Return:\+ the area descriptor on success or NULL on failure.

Function Description:\+ Finds a continuous kernel virtual area by address. It finds the \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} and returns its \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}. Returns the \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} or NULL.

struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structvmap__node}{vmap\+\_\+node}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} find\+\_\+vmap\+\_\+area\+\_\+exceed\+\_\+addr\+\_\+lock (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{addr}{, }\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{va}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

Returns a node where a first VA, that satisfies addr \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<} va\+\_\+end, resides. If success, a node is locked. A user is responsible to unlock it when a VA is no longer needed to be accessed.

Returns NULL if nothing found.

Function Description:\+ Finds a \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} whose end address exceeds the given address, with locking. It searches all nodes and returns the area with the smallest start address. Used for \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_ad9f5fb23122792d02fb4b9a116bb552d}{vread\+\_\+iter()}.

\+\_\+\+\_\+always\+\_\+inline struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} find\+\_\+vmap\+\_\+lowest\+\_\+match (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct rb\+\_\+root \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{root}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{align}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{vstart}{, }\item[{bool}]{adjust\+\_\+search\+\_\+size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

Find the first free block(lowest start address) in the tree, that will accomplish the request corresponding to passing parameters. Please note, with an alignment bigger than PAGE\+\_\+\+SIZE, a search length is adjusted to account for worst case alignment overhead.

Function Description:\+ Finds the lowest free \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} that can accommodate an allocation. It searches the free tree using subtree\+\_\+max\+\_\+size for pruning. Returns the best-\/fit area or NULL.

Return:\+ the area descriptor on success or NULL on failure.

Function Description:\+ Reserves a contiguous kernel virtual area. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_af575bf68e263ce21bbbe0baca21cc979}{\+\_\+\+\_\+get\+\_\+vm\+\_\+area\+\_\+node()} with default parameters. Returns the \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Allocates a \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} for a virtual memory area of the given size and flags. It reserves a region in the vmalloc address space without allocating physical pages. Returns the \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} or NULL.

\Hypertarget{vmalloc_8c_a328f274945d45272f29c4972acd4de81}\index{vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}!lazy\_max\_pages@{lazy\_max\_pages}}
\index{lazy\_max\_pages@{lazy\_max\_pages}!vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{lazy\_max\_pages()}{lazy\_max\_pages()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a328f274945d45272f29c4972acd4de81} 
unsigned long lazy\+\_\+max\+\_\+pages (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

lazy\+\_\+max\+\_\+pages is the maximum amount of virtual address space we gather up before attempting to purge with a TLB flush.

There is a tradeoff here:\+ a larger number will cover more kernel page tables and take slightly longer to purge, but it will linearly reduce the number of global TLB flushes that must be performed. It would seem natural to scale this number up linearly with the number of CPUs (because vmapping activity could also scale linearly with the number of CPUs), however it is likely that in practice, workloads might be constrained in other ways that mean vmap activity will not scale linearly with CPUs. Also, I want to be conservative and not introduce a big latency on huge systems, so go with a less aggressive log scale. It will still be an improvement over the old code, and it will be simple to change the scale factor if we find that it becomes a problem on bigger systems.

Function Description:\+ Returns the maximum number of pages that can be lazily freed before triggering a purge. It scales with the number of CPUs using a logarithmic factor. Used to amortize TLB flush costs.

\Hypertarget{vmalloc_8c_a003f56d75368f13a3519ddc63f2dd817}\index{vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}!memalloc\_apply\_gfp\_scope@{memalloc\_apply\_gfp\_scope}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{memalloc\_apply\_gfp\_scope()}{memalloc\_apply\_gfp\_scope()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a003f56d75368f13a3519ddc63f2dd817} 
unsigned int memalloc\+\_\+apply\+\_\+gfp\+\_\+scope (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfp\+\_\+mask}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Page tables allocations ignore external GFP. Enforces it by the memalloc scope API. It is used by vmalloc internals and KASAN shadow population only.

GFP to scope mapping:\+

non-\/blocking (no \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+DIRECT\+\_\+\+RECLAIM) -\/ memalloc\+\_\+noreclaim\+\_\+save() GFP\+\_\+\+NOFS -\/ memalloc\+\_\+nofs\+\_\+save() GFP\+\_\+\+NOIO -\/ memalloc\+\_\+noio\+\_\+save()

Returns a flag cookie to pair with restore.

Function Description:\+ Applies memory allocation scope based on GFP flags. It saves the current scope and applies the requested scope. Returns a cookie for restoration.

Function Description:\+ Applies the current memory allocation scope to GFP flags. This adds flags based on the current task\textquotesingle{}s memory allocation context. Returns the modified GFP flags.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{pcpu\_get\_vm\_areas()}{pcpu\_get\_vm\_areas()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a8ac394f9dd55e5dca1705b7d761aca93} 
struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} pcpu\+\_\+get\+\_\+vm\+\_\+areas (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned long \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{offsets}{, }\item[{const size\+\_\+t \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{sizes}{, }\item[{int}]{nr\+\_\+vms}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{align}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

pcpu\+\_\+get\+\_\+vm\+\_\+areas -\/ allocate vmalloc areas for percpu allocator @offsets:\+ array containing offset of each area @sizes:\+ array containing size of each area @nr\+\_\+vms:\+ the number of areas to allocate @align:\+ alignment, all entries in @offsets and @sizes must be aligned to this

Returns:\+ kmalloc\textquotesingle{}d \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} pointer array pointing to allocated vm\+\_\+structs on success, NULL on failure

Percpu allocator wants to use congruent vm areas so that it can maintain the offsets among percpu areas. This function allocates congruent vmalloc areas for it with GFP\+\_\+\+KERNEL. These areas tend to be scattered pretty far, distance between two areas easily going up to gigabytes. To avoid interacting with regular vmallocs, these areas are allocated from top.

Despite its complicated look, this allocator is rather simple. It does everything top-\/down and scans free blocks from the end looking for matching base. While scanning, if any of the areas do not fit the base address is pulled down to fit the area. Scanning is repeated till all the areas fit and then all necessary data structures are inserted and the result is returned.

Function Description:\+ Allocates multiple congruent vmalloc areas for percpu allocator. It scans from the top of the vmalloc space and allocates aligned areas. Returns an array of vm\+\_\+structs.

Function Description:\+ Allocates multiple \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} areas for per-\/\+CPU data. It takes offsets and sizes for each area and creates them. Returns an array of \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} pointers.

remap\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+range -\/ map vmalloc pages to userspace @vma:\+ vma to cover (map full range of vma) @addr:\+ vmalloc memory @pgoff:\+ number of pages into addr before first page to map

Returns:\+ 0 for success, -\/Exxx on failure

This function checks that addr is a valid vmalloc\textquotesingle{}ed area, and that it is big enough to cover the vma. Will return failure if that criteria isn\textquotesingle{}t met.

Similar to remap\+\_\+pfn\+\_\+range() (see mm/\+memory.\+c)

Function Description:\+ Maps a vmalloc area to userspace for the full VMA range. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4cf06feeca551e895c56816fa2aad779}{remap\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+partial()}. Returns 0 on success or a negative error code.

Function Description:\+ Maps a vmalloc area into a user-\/space VMA. It maps the entire vmalloc area starting from the given offset. Returns 0 on success or an error code.

remap\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+partial -\/ map vmalloc pages to userspace @vma:\+ vma to cover @uaddr:\+ target user address to start at @kaddr:\+ virtual address of vmalloc kernel memory @pgoff:\+ offset from @kaddr to start at @size:\+ size of map area

This function checks that @kaddr is a valid vmalloc\textquotesingle{}ed area, and that it is big enough to cover the range starting at @uaddr in @vma. Will return failure if that criteria isn\textquotesingle{}t met.

Function Description:\+ Maps a portion of a vmalloc area to userspace. It validates the area and inserts pages into the VMA. Returns 0 on success or a negative error code.

Function Description:\+ Maps a portion of a vmalloc area into a user-\/space VMA. It creates a page table mapping from the vmalloc area to user space. Returns 0 on success or an error code.

struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} remove\+\_\+vm\+\_\+area (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

remove\+\_\+vm\+\_\+area -\/ find and remove a continuous kernel virtual area @addr:\+ base address

Search for the kernel VM area starting at @addr, and remove it. This function returns the found VM area, but using it is NOT safe on SMP machines, except for its size or flags.

Return:\+ the area descriptor on success or NULL on failure.

Function Description:\+ Finds and removes a continuous kernel virtual area by address. It unlinks the \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} and returns the \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}. Used during vfree.

Function Description:\+ Removes a \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} area from the vmalloc address space. It finds the area by address, removes it from the tree, and returns the \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}. Returns NULL if not found.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vfree()}{vfree()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23} 
void vfree (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vfree -\/ Release memory allocated by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} @addr:\+ Memory base address

Free the virtually continuous memory area starting at @addr, as obtained from one of the \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} family of APIs. This will usually also free the physical memory underlying the virtual allocation, but that memory is reference counted, so it will not be freed until the last user goes away.

If @addr is NULL, no operation is performed.

Context:\+ May sleep if called {\itshape not} from interrupt context. Must not be called in NMI context (strictly speaking, it could be if we have CONFIG\+\_\+\+ARCH\+\_\+\+HAVE\+\_\+\+NMI\+\_\+\+SAFE\+\_\+\+CMPXCHG, but making the calling conventions for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} arch-\/dependent would be a really bad idea).

Function Description:\+ Frees memory allocated by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} or related functions. It removes the vm\+\_\+area, frees physical pages, and releases the virtual address space. This is the main deallocation function for vmalloc.

Function Description:\+ Frees memory allocated with \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} or related functions. It returns the memory to the vmalloc subsystem. This is the main deallocation function for vmalloc memory.

void vfree\+\_\+atomic (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vfree\+\_\+atomic -\/ release memory allocated by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} @addr:\+ memory base address

This one is just like \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} but can be called in any atomic context except NMIs.

Description:\+ Frees vmalloc memory from atomic context. It adds the address to the deferred list and schedules a work. Must not be called from NMI context.

Function Description:\+ Frees vmalloc memory in atomic context. This is a safe version of \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} that can be called from interrupt context. It uses a deferred free mechanism.

vm\+\_\+area\+\_\+add\+\_\+early -\/ add vmap area early during boot @vm:\+ \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} to add

This function is used to add fixed kernel vm area to vmlist before \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_ab07c7ebcaaf28eac4ad5dcd65ea93853}{vmalloc\+\_\+init()} is called. @vm-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}addr, @vm-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}size, and @vm-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}flags should contain proper values and the other fields should be zero.

Function Description:\+ Adds a vmap area early during boot before \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_ab07c7ebcaaf28eac4ad5dcd65ea93853}{vmalloc\+\_\+init()}. It inserts the area into the vmlist. Used for early kernel VM areas.

Internals. Don\textquotesingle{}t use..

Function Description:\+ Adds a \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} area early during boot. This is used for early vmalloc initialization. Called during kernel initialization.

vm\+\_\+area\+\_\+register\+\_\+early -\/ register vmap area early during boot @vm:\+ \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} to register @align:\+ requested alignment

This function is used to register kernel vm area before \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_ab07c7ebcaaf28eac4ad5dcd65ea93853}{vmalloc\+\_\+init()} is called. @vm-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}size and @vm-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}flags should contain proper values on entry and other fields should be zero. On return, vm-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}addr contains the allocated address.

DO NOT USE THIS FUNCTION UNLESS YOU KNOW WHAT YOU\textquotesingle{}RE DOING.

Function Description:\+ Registers a vmap area early during boot. It allocates an address from the vmalloc space and adds it to vmlist. Used for early kernel VM areas.

Function Description:\+ Registers a \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} area early during boot with alignment. This is used for early vmalloc initialization with specific alignment. Called during kernel initialization.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vm\_map\_ram()}{vm\_map\_ram()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_acd02620225f5b290f8935dbc1e45b18e} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct page \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{pages}{, }\item[{unsigned int}]{count}{, }\item[{int}]{node}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram -\/ map pages linearly into kernel virtual address (vmalloc space) @pages:\+ an array of pointers to the pages to be mapped @count:\+ number of pages @node:\+ prefer to allocate data structures on this node

If you use this function for less than VMAP\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+ALLOC pages, it could be faster than vmap so it\textquotesingle{}s good. But if you mix long-\/life and short-\/life objects with \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_acd02620225f5b290f8935dbc1e45b18e}{vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram()}, it could consume lots of address space through fragmentation (especially on a 32bit machine). You could see failures in the end. Please use this function for short-\/lived objects.

Returns:\+ a pointer to the address that has been mapped, or NULL on failure

Function Description:\+ Maps pages linearly into kernel virtual address space (vmalloc space). It uses vmap\+\_\+blocks for small mappings and alloc\+\_\+vmap\+\_\+area for large mappings. Returns the virtual address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Maps a set of pages into a contiguous virtual address space. It takes an array of pages and maps them into RAM space. Returns the virtual address or NULL on failure. 256K with 4K pages

indicates vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram area

\index{vm\_unmap\_aliases@{vm\_unmap\_aliases}!vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vm\_unmap\_aliases()}{vm\_unmap\_aliases()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a80f7345092c599bf5a7648045113ae99} 
void vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+aliases (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+aliases -\/ unmap outstanding lazy aliases in the vmap layer

The vmap/\+vmalloc layer lazily flushes kernel virtual mappings primarily to amortize TLB flushing overheads. What this means is that any page you have now, may, in a former life, have been mapped into kernel virtual address by the vmap layer and so there might be some CPUs with TLB entries still referencing that page (additional to the regular 1:\+1 kernel mapping).

vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+aliases flushes all such lazy mappings. After it returns, we can be sure that none of the pages we have control over will have any aliases from the vmap layer.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps outstanding lazy aliases in the vmap layer. It flushes all pending lazy mappings to ensure no stale TLB entries remain. Used before freeing pages that may have vmap aliases.

Function Description:\+ Forces the unmapping of all pending vmalloc aliases. This flushes any cached mappings that might be pending. Used to ensure all mappings are removed.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps a linear kernel address space set up by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_acd02620225f5b290f8935dbc1e45b18e}{vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram()}. It handles both small (\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}= VMAP\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+ALLOC) and large mappings. Used to free vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram allocations.

Highlevel APIs for driver use

Function Description:\+ Unmaps a RAM region previously mapped with \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_acd02620225f5b290f8935dbc1e45b18e}{vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram()}. It removes the mapping for the given memory and count of pages. This is used to free RAM mappings. 256K with 4K pages

vmalloc\+\_\+node -\/ allocate memory on a specific node @size:\+ allocation size @node:\+ numa node

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator and map them into contiguous kernel virtual space.

For tight control over page level allocator and protection flags use \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_abf4d6ac386bd660cbdd449b5cfefc11d}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc()} instead.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL on error

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory on a specific node. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_ac0437723f499fd249a1859f24eff01d0}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+noprof()} with GFP\+\_\+\+KERNEL. Returns the address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory on a specific NUMA node without allocation profiling. It allocates memory from the specified node. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a3b2d920abf792e259839419e3d968148}{vmalloc\+\_\+node()}.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} vmap (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct page \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{pages}{, }\item[{unsigned int}]{count}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{flags}{, }\item[{pgprot\+\_\+t}]{prot}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vmap -\/ map an array of pages into virtually contiguous space @pages:\+ array of page pointers @count:\+ number of pages to map @flags:\+ vm\+\_\+area-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}flags @prot:\+ page protection for the mapping

Maps @count pages from @pages into contiguous kernel virtual space. If @flags contains VM\+\_\+\+MAP\+\_\+\+PUT\+\_\+\+PAGES the ownership of the pages array itself (which must be kmalloc or vmalloc memory) and one reference per pages in it are transferred from the caller to \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}, and will be freed /\+ dropped when \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} is called on the return value.

Return:\+ the address of the area or NULL on failure

Function Description:\+ Maps an array of pages into virtually contiguous space. It creates a vm\+\_\+area and maps the pages. Returns the virtual address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Maps an array of pages into a contiguous virtual address space. It creates a mapping with the specified flags and protection. Returns the virtual address or NULL on failure.

Function Description:\+ Returns the virtual address of a specific page within a \doxylink{structvmap__block}{vmap\+\_\+block}. It asserts that the resulting address belongs to the same \doxylink{structvmap__block}{vmap\+\_\+block} index as the start address. vmap\+\_\+pages\+\_\+range -\/ map pages to a kernel virtual address @addr:\+ start of the VM area to map @end:\+ end of the VM area to map (non-\/inclusive) @prot:\+ page protection flags to use @pages:\+ pages to map (always PAGE\+\_\+\+SIZE pages) @page\+\_\+shift:\+ maximum shift that the pages may be mapped with, @pages must be aligned and contiguous up to at least this shift.

RETURNS:\+ 0 on success, -\/errno on failure.

Function Description:\+ Maps pages to virtual addresses with cache flushing. This is the main function for mapping pages into kernel virtual address space. Returns 0 on success or a negative error code.

Function Description:\+ Maps a range of pages into a virtual address range. It creates page table mappings for the specified address range using the given pages and page\+\_\+shift. Returns 0 on success or an error code.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vread\_iter()}{vread\_iter()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_ad9f5fb23122792d02fb4b9a116bb552d} 
long vread\+\_\+iter (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct iov\+\_\+iter \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{iter}{, }\item[{const char \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{count}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\doxylink{vmalloc_8h_ad9f5fb23122792d02fb4b9a116bb552d}{vread\+\_\+iter()} -\/ read vmalloc area in a safe way to an iterator. @iter:\+ the iterator to which data should be written. @addr:\+ vm address. @count:\+ number of bytes to be read.

This function checks that addr is a valid vmalloc\textquotesingle{}ed area, and copy data from that area to a given buffer. If the given memory range of \+[addr...addr+count) includes some valid address, data is copied to proper area of @buf. If there are memory holes, they\textquotesingle{}ll be zero-\/filled. IOREMAP area is treated as memory hole and no copy is done.

If \+[addr...addr+count) doesn\textquotesingle{}t includes any intersects with alive \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} area, returns 0. @buf should be kernel\textquotesingle{}s buffer.

Note:\+ In usual ops, vread() is never necessary because the caller should know \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} area is valid and can use memcpy(). This is for routines which have to access vmalloc area without any information, as /\+proc/\+kcore.

Return:\+ number of bytes for which addr and buf should be increased (same number as @count) or \%0 if \+[addr...addr+count) doesn\textquotesingle{}t include any intersection with valid vmalloc area

Function Description:\+ Reads from a vmalloc area to an iterator safely. It handles memory holes, IOREMAP areas, and sparse areas. Returns the number of bytes read.

for /\+proc/\+kcore

Function Description:\+ Reads from vmalloc memory into an iov\+\_\+iter for /\+proc/\+kcore. It copies data from the vmalloc area to the user iterator. Returns the number of bytes read. mark out the \doxylink{structvmap__block}{vmap\+\_\+block} sub-\/type

indicates vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram area

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vrealloc\_node\_align\_noprof()}{vrealloc\_node\_align\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_afbf9756d42bb1e07738a4eea213dba47} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} vrealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{p}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{align}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{flags}{, }\item[{int}]{nid}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vrealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align -\/ reallocate virtually contiguous memory; contents remain unchanged {\ttfamily }:\+ object to reallocate memory for @size:\+ the size to reallocate @align:\+ requested alignment @flags:\+ the flags for the page level allocator @nid:\+ node number of the target node

If {\ttfamily is} NULL, vrealloc\+\_\+\+XXX() behaves exactly like vmalloc\+\_\+\+XXX(). If @size is 0 and {\ttfamily is} not a NULL pointer, the object pointed to is freed.

If the caller wants the new memory to be on specific node {\itshape only}, \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+THISNODE flag should be set, otherwise the function will try to avoid reallocation and possibly disregard the specified @nid.

If \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO logic is requested, callers must ensure that, starting with the initial memory allocation, every subsequent call to this API for the same memory allocation is flagged with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Otherwise, it is possible that \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO is not fully honored by this API.

Requesting an alignment that is bigger than the alignment of the existing allocation will fail.

In any case, the contents of the object pointed to are preserved up to the lesser of the new and old sizes.

This function must not be called concurrently with itself or \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} for the same memory allocation.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory; NULL if @size is zero or in case of failure

Function Description:\+ Reallocates virtually contiguous memory with alignment and node affinity. It preserves existing contents and handles size changes. Returns the new address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Reallocates memory using vmalloc/\+vfree with alignment and node affinity without allocation profiling. It handles resizing of vmalloc-\/allocated memory, preserving contents up to the smaller size. This is the internal implementation for vrealloc with alignment.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vunmap()}{vunmap()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_af115207536ac97045f67feba98e766d5} 
void vunmap (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vunmap -\/ release virtual mapping obtained by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()} @addr:\+ memory base address

Free the virtually contiguous memory area starting at @addr, which was created from the page array passed to \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}.

Must not be called in interrupt context.

Function Description:\+ Frees a virtual mapping created by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}. It removes the vm\+\_\+area without freeing pages. Used to unmap pages mapped with \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps a \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()} mapping and frees the virtual address space. It removes the mapping for the given address. This is the counterpart to \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vunmap\_range()}{vunmap\_range()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a565049476219e919d8f6dd24ef917cd5} 
void vunmap\+\_\+range (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{addr}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{end}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vunmap\+\_\+range -\/ unmap kernel virtual addresses @addr:\+ start of the VM area to unmap @end:\+ end of the VM area to unmap (non-\/inclusive)

Clears any present PTEs in the virtual address range, flushes TLBs and caches. Any subsequent access to the address before it has been re-\/mapped is a kernel bug.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps a range of virtual addresses with cache and TLB flushing. It calls flush\+\_\+cache\+\_\+vunmap(), \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a80a3260928d403f4980a094110e6eac2}{vunmap\+\_\+range\+\_\+noflush()}, and flush\+\_\+tlb\+\_\+kernel\+\_\+range(). This is the main function for unmapping kernel virtual addresses.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps a range of virtual addresses in the vmalloc space. It tears down page table mappings for the specified range.

void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} vzalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{, }\item[{int}]{node}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vzalloc\+\_\+node -\/ allocate memory on a specific node with zero fill @size:\+ allocation size @node:\+ numa node

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator and map them into contiguous kernel virtual space. The memory allocated is set to zero.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL on error

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed virtually contiguous memory on a specific node. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a3b2d920abf792e259839419e3d968148}{vmalloc\+\_\+node()} with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Returns the address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed virtually contiguous memory on a specific NUMA node without allocation profiling. It allocates zeroed memory from the specified node. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a5a98e640b51e42b458cd21d2b92e60f7}{vzalloc\+\_\+node()}.

\Hypertarget{vmalloc_8c_a2dbfc5e80080d5c4b2a4c165c6edf959}\index{vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}!vzalloc\_noprof@{vzalloc\_noprof}}
\index{vzalloc\_noprof@{vzalloc\_noprof}!vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vzalloc\_noprof()}{vzalloc\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a2dbfc5e80080d5c4b2a4c165c6edf959} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} vzalloc\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

vzalloc -\/ allocate virtually contiguous memory with zero fill @size:\+ allocation size

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator and map them into contiguous kernel virtual space. The memory allocated is set to zero.

For tight control over page level allocator and protection flags use \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_abf4d6ac386bd660cbdd449b5cfefc11d}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc()} instead.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL on error

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed virtually contiguous memory. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Returns the address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed virtually contiguous memory without allocation profiling. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} and zeroes the allocated memory. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a5868566242b769d58025e333da614bee}{vzalloc()}.

\Hypertarget{vmalloc_8c_a29aea0a5ea046162b24c4b55d2a62d0c}\index{vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}!free\_vmap\_area\_root@{free\_vmap\_area\_root}}
\index{free\_vmap\_area\_root@{free\_vmap\_area\_root}!vmalloc.c@{vmalloc.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{free\_vmap\_area\_root}{free\_vmap\_area\_root}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8c_a29aea0a5ea046162b24c4b55d2a62d0c} 
struct rb\+\_\+root free\+\_\+vmap\+\_\+area\+\_\+root = RB\+\_\+\+ROOT\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

This augment red-\/black tree represents the free vmap space. All \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} objects in this tree are sorted by va-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}va\+\_\+start address. It is used for allocation and merging when a vmap object is released.

Each \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} node contains a maximum available free block of its sub-\/tree, right or left. Therefore it is possible to find a lowest match of free area.
